## 🔴 CS2 Incident Analysis — Layer-Down Scheduling Stopped Post PR #462

### Issue created for Foreman investigation:
**[APGI-cmy/maturion-isms#483](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/issues/483)** — *REGRESSION: Layer-down scheduling broken post-PR #462 — Foreman investigation required*

---

## 🔍 My Read of the Root Cause (For Foreman to Verify)

I have read the full diff of PR #462. The problem is almost certainly in **`governance-ripple-sync.yml`** and the interaction between it and **`governance-alignment-schedule.yml`**. Here is the precise failure mode:

### The Deduplication Logic That Is Now Suppressing Everything

PR #462 added a **`check_existing`** step to `governance-ripple-sync.yml` that runs **before** any alignment work. It checks:

1. Any open PR with labels `governance` + `ripple` on a `ripple/` branch
2. Any open PR with labels `governance` + `automated` on a `ripple/` branch

If **either** check finds an open PR, it sets `skip=true` — and **every subsequent step in the entire workflow** is gated on `steps.check_existing.outputs.skip != 'true'`.

The critical problem: **PR #475 (`[Ripple] Propagate governance layer-down`) was merged yesterday** — but you noted it was still open during parts of the session. More importantly, the PR #462 deduplication logic is **permanently suppressing new ripple runs as long as any open governance/ripple PR exists** — including the governance PRs currently open (e.g., PR #479, PR #481 while they were open).

### The Specific Failure Path

```
governance-alignment-schedule.yml runs (hourly cron)
  └─► check_existing step runs
        └─► finds any open PR matching governance+ripple OR governance+automated labels
              └─► sets existing_pr → non-empty
                    └─► "Create or Update Alignment PR" step has condition:
                          if: drift_detected == 'true' && check_existing.outputs.existing_pr == ''
                              ↑ THIS IS NOW ALWAYS FALSE
                    └─► ALL downstream work is gated on existing_pr == '' → nothing runs
```

The `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` PR creation step was changed from:
```yaml
if: steps.align.outputs.drift_detected == 'true'
```
to:
```yaml
if: steps.align.outputs.drift_detected == 'true' && steps.check_existing.outputs.existing_pr == ''
```

This means: **as long as PR #479 (Wave 5.6R, DRAFT, still open) or any other governance-labelled PR exists, the scheduler will stand down every single run.** The idempotency guard is too broad — it catches *any* open governance PR, not just a concurrent ripple PR from the *same trigger event*.

### The Second Problem: `align-governance.sh` Write Guard

The 5-minute write guard added to `align-governance.sh` is sound in isolation, but combined with the above, `sync_state.json` is not being updated on scheduled runs — so the schedule has no record of its last run, and the next check may incorrectly infer state.

---

## 📋 Foreman Investigation Checklist (Pre-Populated)

The issue [#483](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/issues/483) asks Foreman to:

1. **Confirm** whether PR #479 (Wave 5.6R DRAFT, still open) has labels `governance` and/or `ripple`/`automated` — if so, it is the live blocker
2. **Check workflow run logs** for `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` since the merge of #462 — look for the `check_existing` step output: does it say `"Existing governance/ripple PR found"`?
3. **Confirm the fix**: The deduplication condition in `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` must be scoped more narrowly — e.g., only check for PRs on the `governance-alignment-auto` branch, not catch-all `ripple/` prefix checks from *other* workflows
4. **Proposed corrective scope**: The `check_existing` in `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` should only block a *new* alignment PR from being created — it should NOT suppress the `align` step itself (drift detection and `sync_state.json` updates). The current structure skips `align` entirely when `existing_pr` is found.

---

> **Note:** This is an automated governance pipeline regression — it does not affect Wave 5.6R (MAT, separate track) or AIMC Wave 3. Both can proceed in parallel. Fix for this should be a targeted PR from the `governance-liaison-isms-agent` or `api-builder` under Foreman supervision per the new hard-stop protocol.

Restrict deduplication to only truly duplicate ripple triggers\n    - Allow scheduled alignment and layer-down runs to proceed even if unrelated PRs exist\n    - Maintain write-churn protection without suppressing the actual propagation logic\n- Attach evidence: logs, workflow run summaries, screenshots, and diffs\n- Document the acceptance criteria for the fix.\n\n## Acceptance\n- Root cause confirmed and explained\n- Corrective plan posted in comments\n- Evidence bundle provided\n- Follow-up PR or rollback ready for review\n\n---\n*Authority: PR #462; MAT Implementation Plan §2.6.5, §4; Governance Ripple Model; POLC/CS2 oversight; foreman-v2 agent supervision*"

labels: ["regression", "governance", "layer-down", "ripple"]

Yes, confirmed — the draft issue preview rendered as an empty spinner (![image1](image1)), which means the tool didn't save it. Here is the full copy-paste version:

---

**Title:**
```
REGRESSION: Layer-down scheduling broken after PR #462 (Deduplication) — Foreman investigation required
```

**Labels:** `regression`, `governance`, `layer-down`, `ripple`

**Assignee:** `foreman-v2` (or investigating agent of your choice)

---

**Body:**

````markdown name=issue-483-body.md
## Background

- PR #462 (https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/pull/462) deduplicated governance ripple PRs to prevent write-churn and overlapping PR creation by scheduled and triggered runs.
- Prior to this change, scheduled layer-down runs occurred reliably on an hourly cron, producing ripple events and alignment artifacts.
- Since the merge, scheduled automation ("layer-down"/ripple propagation) has stopped entirely — no events, PRs, or receipts have been observed.

---

## Suspected Failure Mode (Pre-Diagnosed)

The `check_existing` step added to `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` and `governance-ripple-sync.yml` by PR #462 is **too broad**. It stands down the **entire workflow** if any open PR is found matching:
- labels `governance` + `ripple` on a `ripple/` branch, OR
- labels `governance` + `automated` on a `ripple/` branch

This catches **unrelated open PRs** (e.g., PR #479 Wave 5.6R DRAFT, which may carry `governance`/`ripple` labels) — not just concurrent duplicate ripple events from the same trigger.

### The specific condition that broke scheduling (`governance-alignment-schedule.yml`):

```yaml
# BEFORE PR #462:
if: steps.align.outputs.drift_detected == 'true'

# AFTER PR #462 (broken):
if: steps.align.outputs.drift_detected == 'true' && steps.check_existing.outputs.existing_pr == ''
```

As long as **any** open governance/ripple-labelled PR exists, `existing_pr` is non-empty → this condition is always `false` → **no alignment PR is ever created, and no layer-down propagates**.

### The cascading effect in `governance-ripple-sync.yml`:

Every downstream step is now gated on `steps.check_existing.outputs.skip != 'true'`, including:
- `Record ripple event receipt`
- `Run governance alignment`
- `Collect alignment metadata`
- `Record layer-down receipt`
- `Create governance liaison issue`
- `Create ripple PR`
- `Enable auto-merge`

If `skip=true`, **the entire workflow is a no-op** — no artifact, no receipt, no PR.

---

## Immediate Effects

- No scheduled layer-down runs propagating since merge of PR #462.
- No receipts, drift reports, or `sync_state.json` updates being written.
- Live blocker candidate: **PR #479** (Wave 5.6R, DRAFT, still open) — if it carries `governance` + `ripple` or `governance` + `automated` labels, it is permanently triggering the skip condition on every hourly cron run.

---

## Action Required — Foreman Investigation

1. **Check labels on PR #479** — does it carry `governance` + `ripple` or `governance` + `automated`? If yes, this is the live blocker.
2. **Inspect recent workflow run logs** for `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` — look for the `check_existing` step output. Does it report `"Existing governance/ripple PR found"`?
3. **Confirm the alignment step** (`Run governance alignment`) — is it skipped on every scheduled run?
4. **Propose a corrective fix** scoped to:
   - Restricting deduplication to PRs created by the **same workflow trigger** (not catch-all label matching)
   - Allowing scheduled alignment and `sync_state.json` updates to proceed **regardless** of unrelated open PRs
   - Only blocking *new PR creation* (not the full workflow) when a concurrent ripple PR already exists
5. **Deliver a fix PR** with targeted condition changes to `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` and `governance-ripple-sync.yml`
6. **Attach evidence**: workflow run logs, label inspection of PR #479, before/after diff

---

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Root cause confirmed (file, step, condition identified)
- [ ] Live blocker identified (which open PR is triggering skip)
- [ ] Corrective plan posted as comment before fix PR is opened
- [ ] Fix PR narrows deduplication to same-trigger PRs only
- [ ] Scheduled layer-down runs resume (verified by ≥1 successful cron run post-fix)
- [ ] `sync_state.json` updates resume on scheduled runs
- [ ] No regression to write-churn prevention (deduplication still works for genuine duplicate ripple events)
- [ ] PREHANDOVER proof provided with fix PR

---

*Authority: PR #462; Governance Ripple Model; POLC/CS2 oversight; foreman-v2 agent supervision*
````

---

> **Note on PR #479:** Before assigning this, you may want to quickly check whether PR #479 carries `governance`/`ripple` labels — if it does, simply **removing those labels** from PR #479 would immediately unblock the hourly cron as a temporary relief while the fix is built.

**G-04 — MAT-T-0100 (Evidence Modal Live Data):**
Real assertions verifying `EvidenceCapture` → `EvidenceCollection` delegation with live `useCriterionEvidence` hook:
- `from './EvidenceCollection'` import present ✅

- CST evidence artifact corrected to 133/133 ✅

---

### ⚠️ The Material Concern — G-15 Mobile Viewport Tests

**This is the make-or-break item.**

The PR describes G-15 as: *"Updated stubs with real responsive-class assertions"* and a new `mobile-viewport.test.ts` with 6 dedicated 375px viewport tests asserting CSS class presence (`w-full`, `overflow-x-auto`, `min-w-full`, `truncate`).

**The problem:** These are **static source-analysis tests** — they assert that CSS class strings exist in the source files, not that the UI actually renders correctly at 375px in a browser. The original acceptance criteria for G-15 (MAT Implementation Plan §2.6.9, Task 5.6R.3) explicitly required:

> *"Run Playwright tests at 375px × 812px (mobile) viewport for three critical flows... Assert no horizontal overflow, all interactive elements accessible by touch."*

Asserting `toContain('overflow-x-auto')` in a source file is **not** a Playwright viewport test. It confirms the class is written in code but does not confirm:
- The class is applied to the correct element at runtime
- There is no actual overflow at 375px
- Touch targets are accessible

**This is the same class of "tested ≠ delivered" pattern that caused the original G-15 gap.**

---

### 🔵 Verdict by Gap

| Gap | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| G-03 | ✅ PASS | Real source assertions, 9 checks, correct approach |
| G-04 | ✅ PASS | Live delegation confirmed, mock data absence confirmed |
| G-15 | ⚠️ CONDITIONAL | CSS-class source assertions ≠ Playwright viewport tests per spec |

---

### 🔴 Required Before Merge

**One item only:**

The G-15 tests in `mobile-viewport.test.ts` must use actual viewport rendering — either:
1. **Playwright** at 375px × 812px (as specified in the implementation plan), OR
2. **Vitest + jsdom** with `window.innerWidth = 375` and assertion that overflow is not triggered (acceptable substitute if Playwright is not yet wired)

Source string matching (`toContain('overflow-x-auto')`) alone does not satisfy the acceptance criteria. The agent should add at least one test that simulates a 375px viewport environment and asserts layout behaviour.

---

### 📋 Merge Gate Checklist

- [x] G-03 real assertions ✅
- [x] G-04 live data wiring ✅
- [x] `WAVE_5_6R_EXPLORATION_SUMMARY.md` removed ✅
- [x] BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER reverted correctly ✅
- [x] CST evidence artifact corrected ✅
- [ ] **G-15 viewport tests use actual viewport simulation (not source-string matching)** ← BLOCKER
- [ ] CST count updated after G-15 fix (133 or higher, single run)
- [ ] PREHANDOVER proof updated post-G-15 fix

**Recommend:** Request changes on G-15, approve remainder. Once G-15 viewport simulation is in place, this PR is clean to merge.

6. `PersonaLoader.ts` — needed by AICentre gateway
7. `TelemetryWriter.ts` (full) — needed by AICentre gateway
8. `AICentre.ts` (full gateway) — final integration point
9. `agents/mat-advisor.md` — persona file (can be done in parallel with above)

**Step 5** — Appoint `governance-liaison-isms-agent` to confirm `mat-advisor.md` persona compliance with APS §8.

**Step 6** — Run full test suite. Confirm OPOJD gate.

**Step 7** — Commit wave close evidence bundle (Section 6.3).

**Step 8** — Update `PREHANDOVER_PROOF_TEMPLATE.md` and `BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md` (Section 6).

**Step 9** — Post CS2 advisory review request as PR comment. Tag CS2.

**Step 10** — Mark PR ready for review only after all of the above are complete.

---

*Authority: AAWP v0.1.0, LIVING_AGENT_SYSTEM.md v6.2.0, WAVE_MODEL.md, BUILD_PHILOSOPHY.md, GOV-BREACH-AIMC-W2-001 RCA*
*Issued: 2026-02-24*
````

## Copilot

This is an excellent governance design idea. Let me give you a thorough analysis before we commit to building it.

---

## The Concept: "Fail Only Once" Registry

What you're describing is a **persistent, self-updating error prevention checklist** — a living document that grows with every governance breach, so the same mistake can never happen twice undetected. This is a well-established pattern in safety-critical engineering (aviation checklists, surgical safety protocols) and it maps very well onto how AI agents operate.

---

## How AI Agents Actually Work — Relevant to Your Design

AI coding agents (like `foreman-v2`) operate with:

1. **System prompt / instructions** — loaded at session start. This is the highest-authority layer. If a rule is here, the agent reads it every time.
2. **Knowledge files** — documents in `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/` that the agent is instructed to read during preflight. These are **Tier 2** in your architecture.
3. **Memory files** — session records in `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/` — written after events, read at session resumption.
4. **Labels / triggers** — in GitHub Actions workflows, labels on PRs/issues can trigger specific workflow runs. In agent terms, the equivalent is **condition checks in the preflight protocol** — the agent checks for certain conditions and loads the relevant checklist.

Your instinct about labels is correct for the **workflow automation layer** (e.g., a GitHub Actions workflow triggered by label `governance-breach` could post a reminder comment). For the **agent reasoning layer**, the equivalent trigger is a **keyword or condition in the preflight checklist** that causes the agent to run the relevant section.

---

## Proposed Architecture

### Tier 1 — Foreman Contract (existing)
The foreman agent contract (`FOREMAN_AGENT_CONTRACT.md` or equivalent) already contains the core POLC rules. You add **one new line** here:

> "During preflight, after standard checks, Foreman MUST read and self-attest against `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md` before any wave action."

This is the **trigger hook** — it lives in Tier 1 (highest authority) but delegates to Tier 2 for the actual list.

### Tier 2 — The Registry File
```
.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md
```

This file has two sections:

**Section A — Universal Rules** (always checked, regardless of context)
These are the permanent "I do not / I must" statements written in first-person to maximise agent compliance.

**Section B — Conditional Triggers** (checked when a specific context is detected)
These are rules that only apply in certain situations. The agent checks "does my current task match this trigger?" and if yes, it reads the associated rule.

### Tier 3 — Memory (existing)
Each RCA/incident document in `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/` includes a **`## Learning Loop Entry`** section at the bottom with the exact one-liner to be added to `FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md`. The foreman's session close protocol includes: "append all new Learning Loop Entries to `FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md`."

---

## Proposed File Structure

````markdown name=FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md
# FAIL-ONLY-ONCE Registry
**Location**: `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md`
**Authority**: Foreman Agent Contract v2.2.0+, POLC
**Preflight**: Foreman MUST self-attest against ALL Universal Rules (Section A) and ALL matching Conditional Rules (Section B) before proceeding with any wave action.
**Update Protocol**: Each governance breach RCA must append a new entry. Entries are NEVER removed.
**Version**: 1.0.0 — seeded 2026-02-24

---

## Preflight Self-Attestation

Before proceeding, Foreman reads each rule aloud (in reasoning) and confirms it is not being violated in the current action. If any rule is violated, Foreman STOPS and resolves the violation before continuing.

---

## Section A — Universal Rules (Always Check)

These apply to every wave action, every session, without exception.

### Process & Sequencing
- [ ] A-01: I do NOT raise a wave-close PR unless every AAWP deliverable table row is confirmed PRESENT in the diff.
- [ ] A-02: I do NOT begin implementation before qa-builder has confirmed RED gate tests exist and are failing.
- [ ] A-03: I do NOT self-implement. If a required builder is unavailable, I halt and escalate to CS2.
- [ ] A-04: I do NOT skip the QA-to-Red step on the grounds that "it will be fast" or "tests are obvious."
- [ ] A-05: I do NOT mark a wave complete unless OPOJD gate passes: 0 failures, 0 skipped, 0 warnings, 0 stubs.

### Evidence & Artifacts
- [ ] A-06: I do NOT close a wave without a PREHANDOVER_PROOF artifact committed.
- [ ] A-07: I do NOT allow builder working notes, exploration summaries, or draft files to be committed to the repo root or any non-designated location.
- [ ] A-08: I do NOT fabricate or estimate test results. Evidence must be from an actual test run.
- [ ] A-09: I do NOT mark a test GREEN based on source-string matching alone when the spec requires runtime or viewport verification.

### Governance & Escalation
- [ ] A-10: I do NOT proceed past a CS2 hard-stop gate without recorded CS2 approval.
- [ ] A-11: I do NOT treat a partial delivery as a wave close. A wave is closed when ALL contracted deliverables are present and all tests are GREEN.
- [ ] A-12: I MUST record every governance breach in BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md before the session closes.
- [ ] A-13: I MUST update this file (FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md) with any new learning loop entry before the session closes after a breach.

---

## Section B — Conditional Rules (Check When Trigger Matches)

Read each trigger. If your current context matches, the associated rule is MANDATORY.

| # | Trigger | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| B-01 | I am about to raise a PR | I MUST verify the PR diff contains every file listed in the AAWP deliverable table for this wave before opening the PR. |
| B-02 | I am about to appoint a builder | I MUST confirm qa-builder has already delivered RED gate tests for this wave's scope AND I have confirmed RED before this appointment. |
| B-03 | A builder agent is not responding or unavailable | I MUST halt immediately, record reason in session memory, and escalate to CS2. I do NOT attempt the task myself. |
| B-04 | I am writing or reviewing a CST/CWT evidence artifact | I MUST verify the test count matches the actual run output. I do NOT estimate, round, or copy a prior count without re-running. |
| B-05 | I am closing a wave gate | I MUST run through ALL AAWP Wave close requirements line by line and confirm each is met. I do NOT declare wave close until every item is checked. |
| B-06 | A test uses `expect(true).toBe(true)` or equivalent always-passing assertion | I MUST flag this as a stub and refuse to count it as a passing test. Stubs are test debt. |
| B-07 | I am reviewing a mobile/viewport test | I MUST confirm the test uses actual viewport simulation (Playwright or jsdom with explicit width), not source-string matching of CSS class names. |
| B-08 | I am about to write to a governance canon file | I MUST confirm this change has CS2 approval or is within my delegated authority before committing. |
| B-09 | A ripple/layer-down workflow is being modified | I MUST verify the change does not suppress the alignment step itself — only PR creation may be gated on deduplication logic. |
| B-10 | I am generating a CST checkpoint artifact | I MUST confirm CST protocol was actually executed (cross-subwave API contract validation), not just that individual tests passed. |

---

## Section C — Breach Log

Each entry records when a rule was added and which incident triggered it.

| Rule ID | Added | Incident | Summary |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-01 | 2026-02-24 | Wave 3 PR #483 — incomplete delivery | PR raised with 9 of 10 AAWP Wave 3 deliverables missing |
| A-02 | 2026-02-24 | GOV-BREACH-AIMC-W2-001 | Implementation began without QA-to-Red confirmation |
| A-03 | 2026-02-24 | GOV-BREACH-AIMC-W2-001 | Foreman self-implemented when api-builder was unavailable |
| A-07 | 2026-02-24 | Wave 5.6R PR #479 (original) | WAVE_5_6R_EXPLORATION_SUMMARY.md committed to repo root |
| A-08 | 2026-02-24 | Wave 5.6R PR #479 (original) | CST evidence artifact fabricated — claimed G-15 closed via "responsive class verification" |
| A-09 | 2026-02-24 | Wave 5.6R PR #479 (re-delivery) | G-15 mobile viewport tests used CSS source-string matching instead of Playwright viewport simulation |
| B-06 | 2026-02-24 | Wave 5.6R PR #479 (original) | `expect(true).toBe(true)` stubs submitted as passing tests for G-03, G-04, G-15 |
| B-07 | 2026-02-24 | Wave 5.6R PR #479 (re-delivery) | Mobile viewport test used `toContain('overflow-x-auto')` instead of viewport simulation |
| B-09 | 2026-02-24 | PR #462 regression | Deduplication logic suppressed alignment step, not just PR creation — layer-down scheduling stopped |

---

## Update Protocol (How Foreman Adds Entries)

After any governance breach is confirmed and RCA is complete:

1. Add a new rule to Section A (universal) or Section B (conditional) as appropriate.
2. Write the rule in first-person: "I do NOT..." or "I MUST..."
3. Keep it to one sentence. It must be actionable and unambiguous.
4. Add a row to Section C (Breach Log) with the rule ID, date, incident reference, and one-line summary.
5. Commit the updated file to `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md`.
6. This commit must be part of the same PR as the RCA document.

---

*This document grows with every breach. It never shrinks. Entries are permanent.*
*Authority: Foreman Agent Contract v2.2.0+ | POLC | LIVING_AGENT_SYSTEM.md v6.2.0*
````

One paragraph in the foreman contract's **Preflight Checklist** section:

```
FAIL-ONLY-ONCE Check (mandatory, every session):
Before any wave action, Foreman reads .agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md
in full and self-attests against every Universal Rule (Section A) and every matching
Conditional Rule (Section B). If any rule is violated, Foreman STOPS and resolves before
continuing. After any governance breach, Foreman MUST append a new entry to FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md
as part of the RCA commit.
```

---

## About Labels as Triggers

For the **GitHub Actions layer** (automated workflows), you can add a workflow triggered by the label `governance-breach` that automatically:
1. Posts a comment on the issue/PR pointing to `FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md`
2. Requires Foreman to acknowledge the relevant rule before the PR can proceed

This is a separate (but complementary) enforcement layer to the agent's in-session reasoning check. I can draft that workflow if you want to build it as part of this PR.

| Step | Action | Where |

| 2 | Add the one-paragraph preflight hook to the foreman contract | Same PR |

This keeps it atomic — the registry, the hook, and the first batch of breach entries all land together. Shall I create the issue for this?

````markdown name=fail-only-once-issue.md
## Objective

Create and activate a permanent, self-updating "Fail Only Once" registry for Foreman v2. This is a Tier 2 knowledge document that Foreman MUST read and self-attest against during every session preflight. It grows with every breach. It never shrinks.

**Authority**: CS2 (Johan Ras) | LIVING_AGENT_SYSTEM.md v6.2.0 | foreman-v2 contract v2.2.0

---

## Background

This issue was triggered by a pattern of repeated breach types across multiple sessions and waves:
- Self-implementation when a builder was unavailable (GOV-BREACH-AIMC-W2-001)
- Incomplete wave delivery — PR raised without all AAWP deliverables present (PR #483)
- QA-to-Red step skipped before implementation
- Test stubs (`expect(true).toBe(true)`) submitted as passing tests (Wave 5.6R)
- Builder working notes committed to repo root
- CST evidence fabricated / estimated rather than from actual test run
- CSS source-string matching used instead of viewport simulation for mobile tests
- Deduplication logic suppressed entire governance alignment workflow, not just PR creation (PR #462)

The "Fail Only Once" principle: each breach is added as a permanent, named rule. The same class of error is never undetected again.

---

## Task 1 — Memory Compilation (Foreman executes in POLC-Orchestration mode)

Foreman MUST scan and compile learnings from ALL of the following sources:

### Memory Files (read every file in full):
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-001-20260221.md`
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-002-20260221.md`
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-003-20260221.md`
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-046-20260221.md`
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-047-20260221.md`
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-051-20260223.md`
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-052-20260223.md`
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/memory/session-5.6R-RCA-20260224.md` (if exists)
- All `PREHANDOVER-session-*.md` files
- Any additional session files not listed above

### RCA & Incident Files (read every file in full):
- `governance/rca/GOV_BREACH_AIMC_W2_001_RCA.md`
- Any other files under `governance/rca/`

### Learning & Suggestions Files:
- `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/personal/lessons-learned.md` (if exists)
- All "Suggestions for Improvement" sections in session memory files
- All "Lessons" sections in session memory files

### Tracker:
- `modules/mat/BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md` — scan for all deviation entries, incident records, and governance breach entries

### This Session's Context:
- PR #462 regression: layer-down suppressed by over-broad deduplication
- PR #479 (original): `expect(true)` stubs, fabricated CST, builder notes in repo root
- PR #479 (re-delivery): CSS source-string matching for mobile viewport tests
- PR #483: Wave 3 raised with 9 of 10 AAWP deliverables missing, QA-to-Red not followed

---

## Task 2 — Compile FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md

Using all learnings from Task 1, create:

**File**: `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md`

The document MUST follow this structure exactly:

### Section A — Universal Rules (Always Check)
- Written in first-person: "I do NOT..." or "I MUST..."
- One rule per line, one sentence, unambiguous
- Each rule gets a stable ID: `A-01`, `A-02`, etc.
- Covers: process sequencing, evidence integrity, deliverable completeness, self-attestation, escalation

### Section B — Conditional Rules (Check When Trigger Matches)
- Table format: `ID | Trigger | Rule`
- Trigger is a detectable condition ("I am about to raise a PR", "A builder is unavailable")
- Rule is the mandatory action or prohibition
- Each rule gets a stable ID: `B-01`, `B-02`, etc.

### Section C — Breach Log
- Table format: `Rule ID | Date Added | Incident Reference | One-line Summary`
- Every rule in A and B MUST have a corresponding breach log entry
- Never remove entries

### Header Block
```markdown
# FAIL-ONLY-ONCE Registry
Version: 1.0.0
Seeded: 2026-02-24
Authority: Foreman Agent Contract v2.2.0+ | POLC | LIVING_AGENT_SYSTEM.md v6.2.0
Update Protocol: After every breach RCA, Foreman appends new rule + breach log entry. Never remove. Never skip.
Preflight: Foreman reads this file in full and self-attests at every session start before any wave action.
```

**Minimum expected rules** (Foreman must derive these from memory — additional rules from memory compilation are required):

From breaches to date, these rules MUST be present at minimum:

| Ref | Type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| A-01 | Process | I do NOT raise a wave-close PR unless every AAWP deliverable table row is confirmed PRESENT in the diff. |
| A-02 | Process | I do NOT begin implementation before qa-builder has confirmed RED gate tests exist and are failing. |
| A-03 | Separation | I do NOT self-implement. If a required builder is unavailable, I halt immediately and escalate to CS2. |
| A-04 | Evidence | I do NOT count `expect(true).toBe(true)` or equivalent always-passing assertions as real tests. |
| A-05 | Evidence | I do NOT fabricate, estimate, or copy test counts in evidence artifacts. All counts come from an actual test run. |
| A-06 | Evidence | I do NOT accept mobile/viewport tests that only assert CSS class strings in source files. Viewport tests must use actual viewport simulation. |
| A-07 | Housekeeping | I do NOT allow builder working notes, exploration summaries, or draft files to be committed to repo root or non-designated locations. |
| A-08 | Completeness | I do NOT declare a wave complete unless OPOJD gate passes: 0 failures, 0 skipped, 0 warnings, 0 stubs. |
| A-09 | Memory | I MUST append a new rule to this file as part of every RCA commit. This is non-optional. |
| A-10 | Escalation | I do NOT proceed past a CS2 hard-stop gate without recorded CS2 approval on file. |
| B-01 | Pre-PR | Before raising a PR: I MUST verify every AAWP deliverable table row is present in the diff. |
| B-02 | Pre-appointment | Before appointing a builder: I MUST confirm qa-builder RED gate tests exist and are confirmed RED. |
| B-03 | Builder unavailable | When a required builder cannot be reached: I MUST halt, record reason in session memory, escalate to CS2. I do NOT continue. |
| B-04 | CST/CWT evidence | When writing CST/CWT evidence: I MUST verify test count from actual run output, not from memory or prior artifacts. |
| B-05 | Wave close | When closing a wave gate: I MUST run every AAWP wave close requirement line-by-line before declaring close. |
| B-06 | Workflow modification | When modifying a governance/layer-down workflow: I MUST verify the deduplication logic only gates PR creation, not the alignment/sync step itself. |

Foreman is expected to derive **additional rules** from memory that are not listed above.

After layer-down is confirmed, Foreman MUST appoint `CodexAdvisor-agent` (CS2-gated) to add the following preflight step to the foreman-v2 contract (`.github/agents/foreman-v2-agent.md`), Phase 1 Preflight section:

```markdown
### FAIL-ONLY-ONCE Attestation (mandatory, every session)
Before any wave action, Foreman reads `.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md`
in full and self-attests against every Universal Rule (Section A) and every matching
Conditional Rule (Section B). If any rule is being violated, Foreman STOPS immediately
and resolves the violation before continuing.
After any governance breach, Foreman MUST append a new entry to FAIL-ONLY-ONCE.md as
part of the RCA commit. This step is non-negotiable and cannot be skipped.
```

CodexAdvisor must:

## PR #485 — Fix Layer-Down Scheduling Regression

**Verdict: ✅ APPROVE — Correct fix, safe to merge**

This is a clean, surgically precise fix. The root cause analysis is accurate and the changes are exactly right. Here is the full review:

---

## ✅ `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` — Correct

**Problem fixed:** The two fallback `check_existing` queries that searched `ripple/*` branches have been removed. They were cross-workflow — they belonged to `governance-ripple-sync.yml` and had no business here.

**After this fix:** `check_existing` only searches for PRs on `governance-alignment-*` branches — the stable branch that **this workflow itself owns**. If a ripple PR from an unrelated wave (e.g. PR #479) is open, it is completely invisible to this workflow.

**Key condition preserved correctly:**
```yaml
if: steps.align.outputs.drift_detected == 'true' && steps.check_existing.outputs.existing_pr == ''
```
This still prevents duplicate alignment PRs on the **same** stable branch — deduplication is preserved, just correctly scoped. ✅

---

## ✅ `governance-ripple-sync.yml` — Correct

**Two independent fixes, both correct:**

### Fix 1 — Narrowed `check_existing` JQ filter:
```yaml
# Before (broken): catches ALL ripple/* PRs from any workflow
'.[] | select(.headRefName | startswith("ripple/")) | .number'

# After (correct): only catches PRs from THIS workflow's own branch pattern
'.[] | select(.headRefName | startswith("ripple/governance-sync-")) | .number'
```
This is the exact right scope. The `governance-sync-` prefix uniquely identifies branches created by this workflow. ✅

### Fix 2 — `skip != 'true'` guard lifted from non-PR steps:

| Step | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Record ripple event receipt | gated on `skip != 'true'` | **always runs** ✅ |
| Run governance alignment | gated on `skip != 'true'` | **always runs** ✅ |
| Check diff for agent contracts | gated on `skip != 'true'` | gated on `drift_detected == 'true'` only ✅ |
| Collect alignment metadata | gated on `skip != 'true'` | gated on `drift_detected == 'true'` only ✅ |
| Create escalation document | gated on `skip != 'true'` | gated on `drift_detected + has_agent_files` only ✅ |
| Record layer-down receipt | gated on `skip != 'true'` | gated on `drift_detected == 'true'` only ✅ |
| Create governance liaison issue | gated on `skip != 'true'` | gated on `drift_detected == 'true'` only ✅ |
| Create ripple PR | gated on `skip != 'true'` | **still gated** — correct ✅ |
| Resolve PR number | gated on `skip != 'true'` | **still gated** — correct ✅ |
| Auto-merge | gated on `skip != 'true'` | **still gated** — correct ✅ |

This is precisely the intended behaviour: **all side-effects and artifacts run unconditionally** (or only on `drift_detected`); **only PR creation is idempotency-gated**. ✅

---

## ✅ Deduplication Still Works — No Write-Churn Regression

The original intent of PR #462 (prevent write-churn / duplicate PRs) is **fully preserved**:
- A genuine duplicate ripple PR on `ripple/governance-sync-*` will still set `skip=true` and block PR creation
- Only the cross-workflow false positive is eliminated

---

1. **Hourly cron (`governance-alignment-schedule.yml`)**: Will resume. Drift detection runs, canonical files aligned, `sync_state.json` updated, liaison issue created, alignment PR raised — all unblocked by unrelated open PRs.

3. **FAIL-ONLY-ONCE issue in governance repo**: When its PR is created and merged, the layer-down will fire correctly — receipts written, liaison issue created, ripple PR raised. This is the first live test of the fix.

## ⚠️ Issues — Two Concerns Before Merge

### Issue 1 — MODERATE: jsdom render tests are self-asserting (not component-driven)
This is the key structural limitation. The `mobile-viewport-render.test.ts` tests construct DOM nodes and set `style.overflowX = 'auto'` **themselves** in the test setup, then assert that the same value they just set is `'auto'`. For example:

```typescript name=mobile-viewport-render.test.ts url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/1bec5c1e92b537c7172ece905a98f853bdf99210/modules/mat/tests/mobile-viewport/mobile-viewport-render.test.ts#L103-L108
tabBar.style.overflowX = 'auto'; // ← test sets this
// ...
expect(tabBar.style.overflowX).toBe('auto'); // ← test asserts what it just set
```

These tests will **always pass regardless of what the actual component does**. They verify DOM construction logic, not actual component rendering. A genuine DOM rendering test would use `@testing-library/react` to `render(<EvidenceCollection criterionId="x" />)` and query the rendered output.

**However** — this is the same fundamental constraint as before (no React rendering environment is configured), and the source-analysis tier (`mobile-viewport.test.ts`) genuinely validates the CSS classes in the component files. The jsdom tier adds viewport simulation depth even if it's structurally limited. This is acknowledged in the test file comments.

**CS2 decision required**: Is the two-tier approach (source analysis + jsdom DOM construction) sufficient to close G-15, given the absence of a full `@testing-library/react` render environment? The tests pass and are not stubs — but they are not end-to-end component renders.

## @APGI-cmy

Help me to make the correct decision. Should I accept the two-tier G-15 approach? Keep in mind multi modal enteprise level application with hindereds of thousand if not millions of users

## Copilot

This is the right question to ask, and I'll give you a direct, honest answer.

---

## The Core Question: Is the Two-Tier G-15 Test Approach Production-Grade?

**Short answer: No — not at enterprise scale. But the path to production-grade is defined and achievable.**

---

## What the Two Tiers Actually Test

### Tier 1 — Source Analysis (`mobile-viewport.test.ts`)
✅ **What it proves:** The CSS classes `overflow-x-auto`, `min-w-full`, `w-full`, `truncate` exist in the source files.

❌ **What it does NOT prove:**
- That Tailwind actually applies those classes (Tailwind's JIT purge could strip them if the component isn't in the scan path)
- That the classes apply to the **right** DOM element (a class on a wrapper div ≠ the table being scrollable)
- That browser rendering actually produces scroll behaviour (CSS specificity, conflicting rules, parent `overflow: hidden` can all silently override)
- That a real user on a 375px phone sees a scrollable table, not a clipped/broken one

### Tier 2 — jsdom DOM Construction (`mobile-viewport-render.test.ts`)
✅ **What it proves:** JavaScript DOM manipulation with inline styles works correctly in jsdom.

❌ **What it does NOT prove:**
- The tests construct their **own** DOM with hardcoded inline styles, then assert those same styles. The actual React components are **never rendered**. A bug in `ReviewTable.tsx` would not cause these tests to fail.
- `window.innerWidth = 375` in jsdom has **zero effect on CSS layout** — jsdom has no CSS engine. Media queries, flex, grid, overflow — none of these compute.
- These tests would pass even if every component file was deleted from the repo.

---

## The Enterprise Risk Picture

For an application at hundreds of thousands to millions of users:

| Risk | Impact |
|---|---|
| **CSS class exists but doesn't render correctly** | 100% of mobile users on affected flows get broken UI |
| **Tailwind purge strips the class** | Invisible in dev/CI, explodes in production build |
| **Parent element has `overflow: hidden`** | `overflow-x-auto` on child is silently overridden — scroll never appears |
| **Wrong element gets the class** | Source-analysis test passes, but the wrong div is scrollable |
| **A future refactor moves CSS to a different class or module** | Source-analysis tests still pass, real users break |

The issue is not that the tests are malicious — they're not. The issue is that **they cannot detect these failure modes**. At enterprise scale, even a 0.1% mobile rendering failure rate across millions of sessions is a significant support burden and credibility problem.

---

## What Production-Grade Actually Requires for G-15

The original issue spec said **"Playwright, 3x critical flows"** — that was the correct call. Here is the honest upgrade path:

### Option A — Short-Term Acceptable (Close G-15 conditionally)
Add one genuine component render test using `@testing-library/react`. This does not require Playwright, does not require a browser, and **does not require a running server**. It requires `@testing-library/react` to be installed (likely already in the project or trivial to add):

```typescript
import { render } from '@testing-library/react';
import { ReviewTable } from '../../frontend/src/components/scoring/ReviewTable';

it('ReviewTable renders an overflow-x-auto container', () => {
  const { container } = render(<ReviewTable ... />);
  const wrapper = container.firstChild as HTMLElement;
  // Now asserting on the ACTUAL rendered component, not a manually constructed DOM
  expect(wrapper.className).toContain('overflow-x-auto');
});
```

This is the minimum viable upgrade. It actually renders the components and would catch a refactor that removes the overflow class.

### Option B — Production-Grade (Playwright E2E, as originally specified)
Playwright at 375px with visual screenshots. This catches:
- Tailwind purge issues
- CSS specificity overrides
- Real browser rendering differences
- Actual overflow/scroll behaviour

This is the right long-term target for an enterprise application. It does not have to block the current wave — it can be a Wave 6 pre-condition or a dedicated test infrastructure wave.

---

## My Recommendation to CS2

**Do not accept the current G-15 tests as full gap closure for an enterprise application.**

**However**, here is a pragmatic path that is honest and unblocks the wave:

### ✅ Accept with Conditions — Two-Step Close

**Step 1 (Now — accept PR #479):** Accept the source-analysis tier as **partial evidence** that the implementation exists. Accept the jsdom tier as a placeholder. Mark G-15 as **🔄 PARTIALLY CLOSED — implementation present, render verification pending**.

**Step 2 (Wave 6 pre-condition — block Wave 6 merge until done):** Require one of the following before Wave 6 can start:
- `@testing-library/react` component render tests for the 3 flows (3-5 days work, no new infrastructure), **or**
- Playwright mobile viewport tests (requires Playwright setup, ~1 sprint)

Add a new tracker item: `G-15-VERIFY — Mobile render verification (enterprise gate)`.

---

## The Governance Canon Answer

Your `FULLY_FUNCTIONAL_DELIVERY_STANDARD.md §11` defines "fully functional" — and if it aligns with typical enterprise standards, it requires that tests verify **actual user-facing behaviour**, not just source-file content. By that standard:

> Source strings exist ≠ CSS applies ≠ browser renders correctly ≠ user can scroll on mobile

The two-tier approach closes the **implementation** gap (the code exists) but not the **verification** gap (a real mobile user would not encounter overflow bugs). For an application at your stated scale, those are different things that require different evidence.

**Accept PR #479 as implementation-complete. Do not mark G-15 as fully closed until component render or Playwright tests exist. Gate Wave 6 on it.**

This is a well-constructed adapter. Specifically:

- **GRS-015 compliant**: API key obtained exclusively through `ProviderKeyStore.getKey('github-models')` — no raw `process.env` access in the adapter body ✅
- **GRS-005 compliant**: Every error path — key failure, network failure, HTTP non-200, JSON parse failure — wraps in `ProviderError` ✅
- **AAD §8.2 DI compliant**: `fetchFn` is injectable via constructor, defaulting to global `fetch` in production ✅
- **Wave 3 scope-locked**: `supportedCapabilities = new Set([Capability.ADVISORY])` — does not over-reach into Wave 4+ capabilities ✅
- **`healthCheck()` correct**: Key presence check only — no billable API call ✅
- **Response parsing**: Uses optional chaining `choices[0]?.message?.content ?? ''` — safe against malformed responses ✅

**One minor observation** (not a blocker): The response body is cast with `as { choices: Array<...> }` without runtime validation. If the GitHub Models API returns an unexpected shape, this will silently produce an empty string rather than a `ProviderError`. For an enterprise application, a Zod schema or explicit property check would be more robust. This is Wave 3 acceptable — log it as a Wave 8 hardening item.

### ✅ Implementation Quality

**`OpenAIAdapter.ts`** — Well constructed:

- `timestamp + 1` for assistant turn avoids timestamp collision — good defensive practice
- `sessionId` guard should be verified — the AAWP spec says persistent memory applies "when `sessionId` is present." Check whether the existing implementation has a guard or whether it calls `persist()` unconditionally (a `sessionId`-less call to `persist` could throw or produce orphaned records)

- Analysis routing test: mocked `openai` adapter in config, asserts `capability === ANALYSIS`, `data` is `object`, `providerUsed === 'openai'` — correct

## Root Cause of Deployment Not Running

The workflow is **correctly configured and secrets are in place**. The deployment simply **never triggered** because no PR has ever touched the paths that activate the workflow:

```yaml name=.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/030f33b58f27ed0c0a9aef0b1cf2d157b1152bba/.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml#L7-L17
    paths:
      - 'modules/mat/frontend/**'
      - 'vercel.json'
      - '.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml'
```

**PR #491 only touched governance docs** — `PREHANDOVER_PROOF_WAVE_6_QA.md`, `BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md`, etc. — so the workflow **correctly did not fire**. The agent sent a governance-only PR when what was needed was a frontend-touching PR.

Additionally, looking at the Vercel dashboard (screenshot 3/image2), the **last production deployment was Feb 20** from commit `5d0aa9c` — which was a manual ESLint fix. That means the deployment pipeline has worked before — the Vercel connection is alive. This is just a path-filtering issue.

---

## What Needs to Happen

### Option A — Recommended: `workflow_dispatch` (Cleanest)

## Lint Failure — Root Cause

![image5](image5)

**Error**:
```
modules/mat/frontend/src/components/evidence/PhotoCapture.tsx#L125
Definition for rule 'jsx-a11y/media-has-caption' was not found
```

**What this means**: The ESLint config references the `jsx-a11y` plugin rule `media-has-caption`, but the `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` package is **not installed** (or not installed correctly) in `modules/mat/frontend/`. ESLint can't find the plugin definition, so it fails with exit code 1.

**Good news**: TypeCheck ✅ and Unit Tests ✅ both passed. This is a pure lint configuration issue — not a code logic or test failure.

---

## Fix Required

The agent needs to do **one of two things** (in order of preference):

### Fix 1 — Install the missing plugin (correct fix)
Add `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` to `modules/mat/frontend/package.json` devDependencies:
```bash
npm install --save-dev eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y
```
And ensure the ESLint config has it registered as a plugin.

### Fix 2 — Remove the rule (acceptable if plugin is not used elsewhere)
If `jsx-a11y/media-has-caption` is not intentionally enforced, remove or comment out the rule from the ESLint config at `modules/mat/frontend/.eslintrc.*` or `eslint.config.*`.

---

## Recommended Action

**Raise a PR via the agent** to fix the ESLint lint failure in `modules/mat/frontend/`. The issue is:

- File: `modules/mat/frontend/src/components/evidence/PhotoCapture.tsx` line 125
- Rule: `jsx-a11y/media-has-caption` — plugin not found
- Fix: install `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` as a dev dependency and ensure it is correctly registered in the ESLint config

This is a **Wave 2R blocker** (PhotoCapture implementation) intersecting with the Wave 6 deployment gate. Once this lint error is fixed, the `workflow_dispatch` should proceed all the way through to **Deploy Production**.
